# Agents · M · part 6

933 entries, maxkernel-analyze-gpu-reference to mcp-specialist. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **maxkernel-analyze-gpu-reference**: Reads a GPU kernel (CUDA C++, Triton, cuTile, CuTe DSL, or any other GPU dialect) supplied as a REFERENCE (not as the source being converted) and writes ref/context.md — a structural brief plus per-item optimization notes, each tagged ALGORITHMIC (the idea transfers), STRUCTURAL (the purpose transfers, the value must be re-derived) or NON_PORTABLE (no TPU counterpart). Never writes base.py, never ports anything, never leaves ref/. Dispatched once per reference by maxkernel-worker. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-analyze-gpu-reference.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-analyze-source**: Reads a GPU-kernel PRIMARY source (CUDA, Triton, cuTile, CuTe DSL, or other GPU dialects), writes a deep source-context brief (&lt;language&gt;_context.md) and ports it to a faithful JAX reference base.py. Runs once per run; dispatched by maxkernel-worker only when state.primary.language is "cuda", "triton", "cutile", "cutedsl" or "gpu". A PyTorch primary goes to maxkernel-analyze-torch-source instead; a GPU *reference* goes to maxkernel-analyze-gpu-reference. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-analyze-source.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-analyze-torch-source**: Reads the user's PyTorch primary source, writes a deep context brief (torch_context.md) and ports it to a faithful JAX reference base.py. Runs once per run; dispatched by maxkernel-worker when state.primary.language is "pytorch". ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-analyze-torch-source.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-autotune-planner**: Prepares an autotuning specification (parameterized kernel template + search space) for a Pallas kernel. Part of the MaxKernel loop; dispatched by maxkernel-worker. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-autotune-planner.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-autotune-summary**: Summarizes the results of a JAX/Pallas kernel autotuning sweep. Part of the MaxKernel loop; dispatched by maxkernel-worker. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-autotune-summary.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-compilation-summary**: Summarizes Pallas kernel compilation validation results. Part of the MaxKernel loop; dispatched by maxkernel-worker. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-compilation-summary.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-extract-jax-source**: Writes a self-contained JAX source.py (computation + get_inputs) from JAX code in the wild — a package with relative imports, parameters held in a module library, and no input factory — reproducing one named entry point faithfully, for the user to approve before anything else runs. The approved file becomes base.py unchanged. Never optimizes. Dispatched once by maxkernel-worker in Phase 0.15 when state.primary.extraction is set and state.primary.language is "jax". A PyTorch codebase goes to maxkernel-extract-source instead. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-extract-jax-source.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-extract-source**: Writes a self-contained PyTorch source.py (Model + get_init_inputs + get_inputs) from PyTorch code in the wild — a package with relative imports, GPU-only dependencies and no input factories — reproducing one named entry point faithfully, for the user to approve before anything else runs. Never writes JAX or Pallas, never optimizes. Dispatched once by maxkernel-worker in Phase 0.15 when state.primary.extraction is set and state.primary.language is "pytorch". A JAX codebase goes to maxkernel-extract-jax-source instead. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-extract-source.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-fix-kernel-compilation**: Compiles a JAX/Pallas kernel on the TPU VM and iteratively fixes compilation errors while preserving the optimization strategy. Part of the MaxKernel loop; dispatched by maxkernel-worker. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-fix-kernel-compilation.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-fix-port**: Repairs base.py when verify_port.py finds it disagrees with the golden values captured from the user's source. Edits base.py and nothing else. Dispatched by maxkernel-worker in the Phase 0.8 gate, up to 3 attempts. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-fix-port.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-fix-test-script**: Fixes validation errors in the MaxKernel test harness's get_inputs(). Dispatched by maxkernel-worker when harness validation fails. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-fix-test-script.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-generate-profile-script**: Generates an XProf profiling script for a Pallas TPU kernel. Part of the MaxKernel loop; dispatched by maxkernel-worker. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-generate-profile-script.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-generate-test-file**: Writes get_inputs() -- the one LLM-authored piece of MaxKernel's shared test harness. Runs once per run; dispatched by maxkernel-worker. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-generate-test-file.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-implement-kernel**: Writes and modifies JAX/Pallas TPU kernel code following an approved optimization plan. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-implement-kernel.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-plan-kernel**: Creates or revises a detailed optimization plan for a JAX/Pallas TPU kernel. Part of the MaxKernel loop; dispatched by maxkernel-worker with a run_dir and iteration. - **maxkernel-reconcile-reference**: Decides whether a GPU reference (CUDA, Triton, cuTile, or any other dialect) computes the same thing as the primary, and turns its candidate ideas into a triaged ideas_ledger.json with a trust verdict. Writes no code. Dispatched once per run by maxkernel-worker when a reference exists. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-reconcile-reference.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-summarize-profile**: Analyzes Pallas kernel XProf traces and summarizes bottlenecks and hardware utilization. Part of the MaxKernel loop; dispatched by maxkernel-worker. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-summarize-profile.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-summarize-test-results**: Analyzes MaxKernel TPU correctness/timing test results and provides actionable recommendations. Dispatched by maxkernel-worker. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-summarize-test-results.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-synthesize-baseline**: Writes a baseline implementation from a written specification when the user supplied no source file. Produces a plain, readable reference (PyTorch or JAX) for the user to approve before any optimization begins. Never writes Pallas, never optimizes. Dispatched once by maxkernel-worker in Phase 0.1 when input.type is "specification". ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-synthesize-baseline.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-test-script-validation-summary**: Summarizes MaxKernel's one-time shared test harness validation loop and guides next steps. Dispatched by maxkernel-worker. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-test-script-validation-summary.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-worker**: Runs ONE full iteration (plan -&gt; implement -&gt; compile -&gt; test -&gt; autotune -&gt; profile) of the MaxKernel Pallas TPU kernel optimization loop, coordinating the other maxkernel-* subagents. Dispatched by the maxkernel skill orchestrator, once per iteration, with a run_dir argument. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-worker.md) · ★106)
- **maxkernel-write-jnp-reference**: Writes a readable pure-JAX reference (base.py) from the torchax jaxpr and the PyTorch source. Runs once per run in torchax mode when emit_jnp_reference is true; dispatched by maxkernel-worker in Phase 0.7. Never writes Pallas. ([AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents](https://github.com/AI-Hypercomputer/accelerator-agents/blob/HEAD/maxkernel/claude-code/agents/maxkernel-write-jnp-reference.md) · ★106)

- **maze-architect**: Route planner for perpendicular PR review. - **mcg**: Product management author and coach. Founder and partner of Silicon Valley Product Group (SVPG, 2001). Author of *Inspired: How to Create Tech Products Customers Love* (2008, 2017), *Empowered: Ordinary People, Extraordinary Products* (2020), *Loved: How to Rethink Marketing for Tech Products* (2022 with Lea Hickman), and *Transformed* (2024). Former product leader at HP, Netscape, AOL, and eBay. Trains the product organizations at companies that build products good enough to be missed when they fail. ([punt-labs/ethos](https://github.com/punt-labs/ethos/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/mcg.md) · ★2 · 5 copies)
- **mcgame-plan-implementer**: Use this agent when the user has an approved implementation plan in `docs/plans/*.md` and wants it executed against the Go server codebase. Examples:\n&lt;example&gt;\nContext: The user has finished writing a plan document for a new battle feature and wants it implemented.\nuser: "I just finished docs/plans/2026-04-27_01_BATTLE_REVENGE_SYSTEM.md, please implement it"\… ([luthebao/mcgame](https://github.com/luthebao/mcgame/blob/HEAD/.agents/agents/mcgame-plan-implementer.md) · ★18)
- **mckee**: Story structure editor applying Robert McKee's principles from 'Story.' Tests whether scenes turn, whether there's a controlling idea, whether the gap between expectation and result creates interest, and whether the piece has the structural integrity of a well-designed narrative. ([mvoitko/dotfiles](https://github.com/mvoitko/dotfiles/blob/HEAD/claude/plugins/writing-council/agents/mckee.md))

- **mcode**: The `mcode` harness runs MiniMax Code with the operator's MiniMax login. Pitwall sends the prompt on stdin, selects one registered model explicitly, sets the dispatch workspace with `--cwd`, and requests JSON output so the answer and token usage are recorded in the run. `MCODE_BIN` overrides binary discovery. ([Buckeyes22/pitwall](https://github.com/Buckeyes22/pitwall/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/mcode.md))
- **mcode-shim**: Dispatches a one-shot prompt to MiniMax Code (MiniMax account; model via --model provider/model; prompt delivered on stdin). ([Buckeyes22/pitwall](https://github.com/Buckeyes22/pitwall/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude/agents/mcode-shim.md))
- **mcos-architect**: Use proactively to review MCOS architectural changes for phase scope, gateway-first design, and provider-removal correctness. ([flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server](https://github.com/flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/mcos-architect.md))
- **mcos-code-reviewer**: Use after a logical chunk of work is done, before commit, or whenever the user asks to "review my changes", "check the diff", "is this PR-ready", or "review recent code". Reviews the unstaged + staged diff against MCOS realignment rules and reports findings in confidence order. Trigger proactively after Edit/Write tool runs. ([flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server](https://github.com/flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/mcos-code-reviewer.md))
- **mcos-contract-auditor**: Use after a phase commit, before declaring a phase complete, or whenever the user asks to "verify contracts", "run forbidden grep", "check Forsetti compliance", or "audit the realignment". Runs the FORBIDDEN-CONTRACT grep list (via the mcos-contracts MCP server) plus the Forsetti compliance script and reports structured pass/fail. Use proactively after large diffs in src/, resources/web/, .github/workflows/, or installer/. ([flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server](https://github.com/flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/mcos-contract-auditor.md))
- **mcos-debugger**: Use when a build fails, a ctest target fails, the runtime crashes or misbehaves, or the user reports a bug. Diagnoses failures by reading source, build logs, and test output, then reports root cause with a minimal-blast-radius fix proposal. Triggers on phrases like "build failed", "test broke", "crashes on", "wrong output", "regression", "broken since". ([flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server](https://github.com/flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/mcos-debugger.md))
- **mcos-governance-reviewer**: Use to review pending governance approvals, surface CLU posture, examine governance bundles, and walk the operator through approve/reject decisions. Triggers on phrases like "what's in the approval queue", "should I approve this CLU action", "show me the governance bundle for windows", "why is posture blocked". ([flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server](https://github.com/flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server/blob/HEAD/.claude-plugin/mcos-control/agents/mcos-governance-reviewer.md))
- **mcos-installer**: Use to bring a fresh MCOS instance up to working state — register pools, configure the gateway, set the instance label, validate LAN advertising. Triggers on phrases like "set up MCOS", "configure a fresh install", "get this working on the LAN", "register my first pool". ([flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server](https://github.com/flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server/blob/HEAD/.claude-plugin/mcos-control/agents/mcos-installer.md))
- **mcos-operator**: Use for live MCOS operations on a running instance — health checks, applying configuration changes, pool management, governance approvals, log inspection. Drives the running server through the mcos-bridge MCP server. Triggers on phrases like "what's the status of MCOS", "show me activity", "drain that pool", "approve the pending governance action", "the dashboard says X — fix it". ([flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server](https://github.com/flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server/blob/HEAD/.claude-plugin/mcos-control/agents/mcos-operator.md))
- **mcos-phase-planner**: Use at the start of a new phase, when the user says "proceed" / "start PHASE-XX", or asks "what's the plan for the next phase". Reads the phase file and its `readFirst` files, inventories the existing surface, and produces a file-by-file edit plan in the same shape used by every prior phase. Read-only — does not edit anything. ([flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server](https://github.com/flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/mcos-phase-planner.md))
- **mcos-pool-architect**: Use when designing or sizing managed worker pools — picking minInstances/maxInstances, drain semantics, health probes, kind selection (mcp-server vs sub-agent), and analyzing existing pool saturation patterns. Triggers on phrases like "what scale policy should I use", "this pool keeps hitting maxInstances", "should this be an mcp-server or a sub-agent". ([flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server](https://github.com/flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server/blob/HEAD/.claude-plugin/mcos-control/agents/mcos-pool-architect.md))
- **mcos-researcher**: Use for "where is X defined", "what consumes Y", "find all call sites of Z", "what does this file actually do", or any open-ended codebase recon question. Surfaces facts about the existing implementation so the main session can plan accurately. Read-only. ([flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server](https://github.com/flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/mcos-researcher.md))
- **mcos-troubleshooter**: Use when MCOS is misbehaving — service not running, gateway unhealthy, LAN clients can't discover, pools failing to start, telemetry empty, governance posture stuck. Drives the diagnosis chain through the mcos-bridge MCP tools and reports root cause with a minimal-blast-radius fix proposal. ([flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server](https://github.com/flynn33/Master-Control-Orchestration-Server/blob/HEAD/.claude-plugin/mcos-control/agents/mcos-troubleshooter.md))
- **mcp**: Studio exposes your team's datasets, storages, knowledge base, and jobs over [MCP (Model Context Protocol)](https://modelcontextprotocol.io). Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, and other MCP clients connect to it. The agent acts with your Studio permissions and can run DataChain jobs on the team's clusters. See [Tools](mcp-tools.md) for what it can do. ([datachain-ai/datachain](https://github.com/datachain-ai/datachain/blob/HEAD/docs/studio/user-guide/agents/mcp.md) · ★2.8k)

- **mcp**: An **MCP server** is a separate program that gives the agent tools it would not otherwise have. MCP stands for Model Context Protocol, and the idea is simple: the server advertises a set of named tools, the agent calls them, the server does the work and returns a result. Without one, the agent has a shell and a text editor. With one, it can drive a browser, drive a phone, or read a GitHub issue. ([cybersemics/em](https://github.com/cybersemics/em/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/mcp.md) · ★358)
- **mcp**: Cydonia as an MCP server, and the MCP servers it offers the agents it runs. ([crabtalk/cydonia](https://github.com/crabtalk/cydonia/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/mcp.md) · ★207)
